The authors are grateful to Loïc Poulain d’Andecy for enlightening discussions, relevant observations and pointing out useful references. They also acknowledge helpful conversations with Julien Gaboriaud. NC thanks the CRM for its hospitality and is supported by the international research projects AAPT of the CNRS. The work of LV is supported by a Discovery Grant from the Natural Sciences and Engineering Research Council (NSERC) of Canada. MZ holds an Alexander–Graham–Bell graduate scholarship from NSERC.

Appendix A
Proof of Proposition
5.1

This appendix presents a direct proof of Proposition
5.1
.

From the definition (
5.4
) of the coproduct of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
, one finds
\Delta(\mu)=\Delta^{\text{op}}(\mu)=\mu\otimes\mu
. One also finds from the property (
5.7
) that
\mathcal{R}\Delta(\mu)=\Delta^{\text{op}}(\mu)\mathcal{R}
. These two facts together with the action (
5.29
) of the permutation operator and the definition (
5.30
) imply equation (
5.37
).

For
m,n=-j,...,j
, one can write

\bra{j,m}\text{Tr}_{1}^{(j)}(\check{\mathcal{R}}(\mu\otimes 1))\ket{j,n}=\sum_{\ell=-j}^{j}\bra{j,m;j,\ell}\mathcal{R}(\mu\otimes 1)\ket{j,\ell;j,n}.

(A.1)

Using the explicit form (
5.10
) of the universal
R
-matrix, the orthogonality and the completeness relation (
5.12
) of the basis vectors, and the fact that
\bra{j,m}H\ket{j,n}=n\delta_{mn}
as seen from (
5.15
), one can write the RHS of the previous equation as

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=-j}^{j}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(q-q^{-1})^{k}}{[k]_{q}!}q^{-k(k+1)/2}

\displaystyle\bra{j,m}F^{k}\ket{j,\ell}\bra{j,\ell}E^{k}\ket{j,n}q^{2\ell n+(k+2)\ell-kn}.

(A.2)

For
k=0,1,...
, one can show by induction from the actions (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) the following

\displaystyle\bra{j,m}E^{k}\ket{j,n}=\begin{cases}\frac{[j-n]_{q}!}{[j-n-k]_{q}!}&\text{if }m-n=k,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(A.3)

\displaystyle\bra{j,m}F^{k}\ket{j,n}=\begin{cases}\frac{[j+n]_{q}!}{[j+n-k]_{q}!}&\text{if }n-m=k,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(A.4)

Hence the only non-zero terms in the sum (
A.2
) are such that
m=n
and
\ell=m+k
, and this sum becomes

\delta_{mn}\sum_{k=0}^{j-m}\frac{(q-q^{-1})^{k}}{[k]_{q}!}q^{-k(k+1)/2}\frac{[j+m+k]_{q}!\ [j-m]_{q}!}{[j+m]_{q}!\ [j-m-k]_{q}!}q^{2(m+k)m+(k+2)(m+k)-km}.

(A.5)

In terms of
q
-Pochhammer symbols (see
[
20
]
for instance), this can be rewritten as

\delta_{mn}q^{2m(m+1)}\sum_{k=0}^{j-m}(-1)^{k}q^{k(k+1+2m-2j)}\frac{(q^{2};q^{2})_{j-m}(q^{2};q^{2})_{j+m+k}}{(q^{2};q^{2})_{k}(q^{2};q^{2})_{j-m-k}(q^{2};q^{2})_{j+m}}.

(A.6)

Using the following two identities
[
20
]

\frac{(q;q)_{n}}{(q;q)_{n-k}}=(q^{-n};q)_{k}(-1)^{k}q^{nk-\binom{k}{2}},\qquad\frac{(a;q)_{n+k}}{(a;q)_{n}}=(aq^{n};q)_{k},

(A.7)

one can write (
A.6
) as

\delta_{mn}q^{2m(m+1)}\sum_{k=0}^{j-m}\frac{(q^{-2(j-m)};q^{2})_{k}(q^{2(j+m+1)};q^{2})_{k}}{(q^{2};q^{2})_{k}}q^{2k}.

(A.8)

Finally, by applying to (
A.8
) the
q
-hypergeometric function formula
[
20
]

{}_{2}\phi_{1}\biggl(\genfrac{.}{.}{0.0pt}{}{q^{-n},b}{c};q,q\biggr)=\frac{(c/b;q)_{n}}{(c;q)_{n}}b^{n}

(A.9)

with the substitutions
q\to q^{2}
,
n=j-m
,
b=q^{2(j+m+1)}
and
c=0
, one gets

\bra{j,m}\text{Tr}_{1}^{(j)}(\check{\mathcal{R}}(\mu\otimes 1))\ket{j,n}=q^{2j(j+1)}\delta_{mn},

(A.10)

which proves the case with positive exponents of (
5.38
). A similar procedure proves the same case for (
5.39
). Note that if one already knows (from previous results, see
[
17
,
21
,
49
]
for instance) that the quantum partial trace in (
A.1
) is central, then the proof presented above simplifies by choosing to evaluate expression (
A.1
) for
m=n=j
only.

To obtain the case with negative exponents in (
5.38
), one can first write

\check{\mathcal{R}}^{-1}(\mu\otimes 1)=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(1\otimes\mu)\Pi_{12}.

(A.11)

The inverse of the universal
R
-matrix is given by (
5.11
). Using the definition (
5.6
) of the antipode
S
and the explicit expression
(
5.10
)
of the universal
R
-matrix, one finds that
\mathcal{R}^{-1}
is the same as
\mathcal{R}
with the replacement
q\to{q^{-1}}
. It is also obviously true that the inverse of
\mu=q^{2H}
is the same as
\mu
with the replacement
q\to{q^{-1}}
. Therefore, one finds from (
A.11
)

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}^{-1}(\mu\otimes 1)\right)(q)=\left(\Pi_{12}\check{\mathcal{R}}(1\otimes\mu^{-1})\Pi_{12}\right)({q^{-1}}),

(A.12)

where the dependence in
q
has been explicitly written. As a consequence, one gets

\text{Tr}_{1}^{(j)}(\check{\mathcal{R}}^{-1}(\mu\otimes 1))=\text{Tr}_{2}^{(j)}(\check{\mathcal{R}}(1\otimes\mu^{-1}))({q^{-1}})=q^{-2j(j+1)}\text{id}.

(A.13)

The case with negative exponents in (
5.39
) is obtained similarly.

Appendix B
Askey–Wilson relations from partial traces of
R
-matrices

In this appendix, we present an alternative proof of the fact that the intermediate Casimir elements of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
satisfy the three Askey–Wilson relations (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) using quantum partial traces of
R
-matrices. Note that throughout this appendix, we will often make use of the Yang–Baxter relation in the form of equations (
5.19
)–(
5.21
) and (
5.23
)–(
5.25
) without stating it explicitly.

B.1
Intermediate Casimir elements in terms of partial traces

The goal of this subsection is to write each of the intermediate Casimir elements of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})^{\otimes 3}
as the quantum trace on the first space of a product of universal
R
-matrices. The first space, which is traced out in the spin-
1/2
representation, will be labeled by
a
, and the others will be labeled by numbers
1,2,3
.

For the elements
Q_{1}
and
Q_{12}
, this is already done in Proposition
5.2
and Corollary
5.1
(one must simply add some tensor factors of
1
on the right if necessary). The element
Q_{123}
can be obtained in a similar manner, by applying
\text{id}\otimes\Delta
on equation (
5.50
). For reference, we write here these elements in terms of the matrices
L^{\pm}
(which we recall are universal
R
-matrices with the first factor represented in the spin-
1/2
) and the spin-
1/2
matrix representation
M
of the element
\mu
which acts only on the traced space
a
:

\displaystyle Q_{1}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{-}_{a1}M_{a}),

(B.1)

\displaystyle Q_{12}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{-}_{a2}L^{-}_{a1}M_{a}),

(B.2)

\displaystyle Q_{123}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L^{-}_{a2}L^{-}_{a1}M_{a}).

(B.3)

The following proposition and its corollary allow to also write the elements
Q_{2}
and
Q_{23}
as partial traces with the traced space
a
on the first position.

Proposition B.1
.

The following equation of operators acting on two
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
-representations holds:

Q_{2}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a}).

(B.4)

Proof.

Using the fact that
Q
is central in
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
and the result of Proposition
5.2
, one finds

\displaystyle Q_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{12}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a2}L^{-}_{a2}M_{a})\mathcal{R}_{12}

(B.5)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{12}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a2}L^{-}_{a2}\mathcal{R}_{12}L_{a1}^{+}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a})

(B.6)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{12}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a1}\mathcal{R}_{12}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a})

(B.7)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a}).

(B.8)

∎

Corollary B.1
.

The following equation of operators acting on three
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
-representations holds:

Q_{23}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a}).

(B.9)

Proof.

Apply
(\text{id}\otimes\Delta)
to equation (
B.4
). Then, on the RHS of the resulting equation, use the fact that
\Delta
is a homomorphism and the properties (
5.26
).
∎

The partially closed braids associated to the algebraic expressions (
B.4
) and (
B.9
) are illustrated in Figure
B.1
at the end of this appendix. Note that one could proceed similarly for the element
Q_{3}
, but this will actually not be useful for deriving the Askey–Wilson relations later.

Finally, we will need the following proposition to express the intermediate Casimir elements associated to the recoupling of the factors
1
and
3
of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})^{\otimes 3}
.

Proposition B.2
.

The following equations of operators acting on three
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
-representations hold:

\displaystyle Q_{13}=

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}^{-1}Q_{12}\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}(L_{a2}^{+})^{-1}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a}),

(B.10)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{13}=

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}Q_{12}\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}^{-1}=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}(L^{-}_{a2})^{-1}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L^{-}_{a2}L^{-}_{a1}M_{a}).

(B.11)

Proof.

One may use the expression (
B.2
) and then proceed as indicated below:

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}^{-1}Q_{12}\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}

\displaystyle=\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{-}_{a2}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}

(B.12)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{23}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\mathcal{R}_{23}

(B.13)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{23}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}\mathcal{R}_{23}L^{+}_{a2}(L_{a2}^{+})^{-1}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})

(B.14)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{23}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a3}L^{+}_{a2}\mathcal{R}_{23}L^{-}_{a3}(L_{a2}^{+})^{-1}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})

(B.15)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{23}^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}\mathcal{R}_{23}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}(L_{a2}^{+})^{-1}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})

(B.16)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}(L_{a2}^{+})^{-1}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a}),

(B.17)

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}Q_{12}\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L_{a2}^{-}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\check{\mathcal{R}}_{23}^{-1}

(B.18)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{32}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a3}L_{a3}^{-}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\mathcal{R}_{32}^{-1}

(B.19)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}(L^{-}_{a2})^{-1}L^{-}_{a2}\mathcal{R}_{32}L^{+}_{a3}L_{a3}^{-}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\mathcal{R}_{32}^{-1}

(B.20)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}(L^{-}_{a2})^{-1}L^{+}_{a3}\mathcal{R}_{32}L^{-}_{a2}L_{a3}^{-}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\mathcal{R}_{32}^{-1}

(B.21)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}(L^{-}_{a2})^{-1}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L_{a2}^{-}\mathcal{R}_{32}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a})\mathcal{R}_{32}^{-1}

(B.22)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}(L^{-}_{a2})^{-1}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L_{a2}^{-}L_{a1}^{-}M_{a}).

(B.23)

∎

The partially closed braids associated to the algebraic expressions (
B.10
) and (
B.11
) are illustrated in Figure
B.2
.

B.2
Recovering the Askey–Wilson relations

In this subsection, we derive the Askey–Wilson relations (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) with the method of partial traces of (universal)
R
-matrices.

Using the expressions (
B.2
) and (
B.9
) of the previous subsection and the fact that
Q_{12}
commutes with the diagonal action of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
in
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})\otimes U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
, one can write the product
Q_{12}Q_{23}
as follows:

\displaystyle Q_{12}Q_{23}

\displaystyle=Q_{12}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a})

(B.24)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}Q_{12}L^{-}_{a3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a})

(B.25)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{a3}\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})L^{-}_{a3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a})

(B.26)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{ab}(R_{ba}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}R_{ba}^{-1}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.27)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}R_{ba}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}R_{ba}^{-1}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b}).

(B.28)

The partially closed braid corresponding to (
B.28
) is illustrated in Figure
B.3
.

In order to recover the AW relations, the idea is to simplify the two blue crossings which appear in the braid of Figure
B.3
. Algebraically, these two crossings correspond to the
R
-matrices acting on the spaces
a
and
b
in (
B.28
). In two spins-
1/2
representations, one can verify that the braided
R
-matrix
\check{R}=\Pi_{12}R
and its inverse can be written as

\check{R}=q^{\frac{1}{2}}-q^{-\frac{1}{2}}P,\qquad\check{R}^{-1}=q^{-\frac{1}{2}}-q^{\frac{1}{2}}P,

(B.29)

where

P=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0\\
0&{q^{-1}}&-1&0\\
0&-1&q&0\\
0&0&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

(B.30)

It is easy to verify that the matrix
P
satisfies

\displaystyle P^{2}=(q+{q^{-1}})P,

(B.31)

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{1}(P(M\otimes\mathbb{I}_{2}))=\mathbb{I}_{2}.

(B.32)

The following propositions are also useful.

Proposition B.3
.

Denote an element of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})\otimes U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
with the first factor represented in the spin
1/2
representation by
F=F^{\alpha}\otimes f_{\alpha}
(a sum with respect to
\alpha
is understood).
Then the following equation holds

P_{12}F_{23}P_{12}=P_{12}\text{Tr}_{a}(F_{a3}M_{a}).

(B.33)

Proof.

Write

P_{12}F_{23}P_{12}=(P\otimes 1)(\mathbb{I}_{2}\otimes F^{\alpha}\otimes f_{\alpha})(P\otimes 1)=P(\mathbb{I}_{2}\otimes F^{\alpha})P\otimes f_{\alpha}.

(B.34)

Suppose that the matrix
F^{\alpha}
is given by

F^{\alpha}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}.

(B.35)

Using the explicit matrix representations (
B.30
) for
P
, (
B.35
) for
F^{\alpha}
and (
5.27
) for
M
, it is easy to verify that

P(\mathbb{I}_{2}\otimes F^{\alpha})P=(qa+{q^{-1}}d)P=\text{Tr}(F^{\alpha}M)P.

(B.36)

Hence

P_{12}F_{23}P_{12}=\text{Tr}(F^{\alpha}M)P\otimes f_{\alpha}=P\otimes\text{Tr}(F^{\alpha}M)f_{\alpha}=P_{12}\text{Tr}_{a}(F_{a3}M_{a}).

(B.37)

∎

Proposition B.4
.

The following equations hold

\displaystyle P_{12}

\displaystyle=L^{+}_{23}L^{+}_{13}P_{12}(L^{+}_{13})^{-1}(L^{+}_{23})^{-1},

(B.38)

\displaystyle=(L^{-}_{23})^{-1}(L^{-}_{13})^{-1}P_{12}L^{-}_{13}L^{-}_{23},

(B.39)

\displaystyle=L^{+}_{23}L^{+}_{13}P_{12}L^{-}_{13}L^{-}_{23}.

(B.40)

Proof.

It is straightforward to verify these relations using the explicit matrix representations (
5.17
), (
5.18
) and (
B.30
). Alternatively, (
B.38
) and (
B.39
) can be proven algebraically by using (
B.29
) and the Yang–Baxter equations for
L^{\pm}
.
∎

One can use (
B.29
) to write the partial trace (
B.28
) as follows

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}R_{ba}L^{+}_{a3}L^{-}_{a3}R_{ba}^{-1}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}\check{R}_{ab}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}\check{R}_{ab}^{-1}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.42)

\displaystyle-q

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}P_{ab}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle-q^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}P_{ab}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b}).

We will now show that each of the terms in equation (
B.42
) can be written using the intermediate Casimir elements of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{su}_{2})
.

The trace in the first term of (
B.42
) is simplified as follows:

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}\text{Tr}_{a}(L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}M_{a})L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}Q_{2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{2}\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{2}Q_{123}.

(B.47)

In the previous equations, we used the expressions (
B.3
) and (
B.4
) and the the fact that
Q_{2}
is central. The partially closed braid corresponding to the algebraic partial trace expression for
Q_{2}Q_{123}
is illustrated in Figure
B.4
.

The trace in the second term of (
B.42
) is simplified by using (
B.40
), (
B.38
) and (
B.32
) as indicated:

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}P_{ab}L_{a2}^{-}L_{b2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}(L_{b2}^{+})^{-1}P_{ab}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}(L_{b2}^{+})^{-1}(L_{b1}^{+})^{-1}P_{ab}L_{b1}^{+}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}(L_{b2}^{+})^{-1}(L_{b1}^{+})^{-1}\text{Tr}_{a}(P_{ab}M_{a})L_{b1}^{+}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}(L_{b2}^{+})^{-1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{13}.

(B.52)

The trace in the third term of (
B.42
) can be simplified in a similar manner by using the results (
B.40
), (
B.38
) and (
B.32
):

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{a1}P_{ab}(L^{-}_{b2})^{-1}(L^{-}_{a2})^{-1}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(P_{ab}L^{+}_{b1}(L^{-}_{b2})^{-1}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(\text{Tr}_{a}(P_{ab}M_{a})L^{+}_{b1}(L^{-}_{b2})^{-1}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}(L^{-}_{b2})^{-1}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})

(B.56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{13}.

(B.57)

Finally, the trace in the fourth term of (
B.42
) is simplified with the help of equations (
B.33
), (
B.40
), (
B.38
) and (
B.32
):

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}L^{+}_{b3}L^{-}_{b3}P_{ab}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}\text{Tr}_{c}(L^{+}_{c3}L^{-}_{c3}M_{c})L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}Q_{3}L_{a2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b2}^{-}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})

(B.59)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{a1}L^{+}_{b2}L^{+}_{a2}P_{ab}L_{a2}^{-}L_{b2}^{-}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})Q_{3}

(B.60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(L^{+}_{b1}L^{+}_{a1}P_{ab}(L_{a1}^{+})^{-1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})Q_{3}

(B.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{ab}(P_{ab}L^{+}_{b1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{a}M_{b})Q_{3}

(B.62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(\text{Tr}_{a}(P_{ab}M_{a})L^{+}_{b1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})Q_{3}

(B.63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{b}(L^{+}_{b1}L_{b1}^{-}M_{b})Q_{3}

(B.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{1}Q_{3}.

(B.65)

Note that we also used expression (
B.1
) and the fact that
Q_{3}
is central.

Therefore, by combining the previous results, we have found

Q_{12}Q_{23}=Q_{2}Q_{123}-qQ_{13}-{q^{-1}}\widetilde{Q}_{13}+Q_{1}Q_{3}.

(B.66)

One can proceed similarly for the product
Q_{23}Q_{12}
, the difference being that the blue crossings in Figure
B.3
have to be inverted. From (
B.29
), we see that this corresponds to taking
q\to{q^{-1}}
in (
B.66
). From this results, the Askey–Wilson relation (
3.6
) is recovered. The relations (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) can be obtained by conjugations of (
3.6
) with universal
R
-matrices, as shown in
[
9
]
.

:

Q_{2}
and
Q_{23}
.

\qquad\widetilde{Q}_{13}\rightarrow\

:

Q_{13}
and
\widetilde{Q}_{13}
.

:

Q_{12}Q_{23}
.

Q_{2}Q_{123}
.
